# Acceptance contract

Version 3, 2026-09-24 (version 2 2026-09-05; version 1 earlier that day; section 23 and the amendments listed under Changes were added at 13:50 EDT on 2026-09-05; sections 24 to 30 on 2026-09-24). This document binds milestones 1 to 3 of `plan.md` and milestone 9, the desktop workspace (sections 24 to 30); sections 23 to 30 name the milestone each clause is first tested in. Code is built against it; tests cite its clauses by number (for example `C-5.4`). A clause changes only by editing this file in the same commit as the code and tests that depend on it. Where this file and `plan.md` disagree, this file wins.

Language: Python 3.12 or newer, standard library only. Packaging with `uv`. No bash runners; the one bash file is the never-rules guard hook, copied byte for byte from v1.

## Changes in version 3

- PR #131 round four (2026-10-06), C-5.5, C-5.7, C-5.11: detached descendants are retained by identity and group, canonical cwd is scanned once per full census, and either visible marker holds. macOS platform binaries and rewritten titles can hide environment markers; the residual requires a writer never observed by lineage/group or cwd, with hidden or removed markers. Legacy kill-owned provider identities can discharge missing child publication after verified death. Only the guardian's launch identity establishes a provider parent link; another census identity at a reused guardian PID cannot do so. These changes supersede round three's residual and legacy blanket hold. Census roots never grant signal authority.
- C-6.11 (2026-10-08, PR #148 CI fixes): output keys in holds and lease queues use C-6.5's comparison identity even when a legacy raw alias supplies the guard. Recorded output paths retain their display spelling.
- C-6.5, C-26.3, C-26.13 (2026-10-06, canonical resource identity; audit L1–L3): output ownership compares resolved folder identity followed by canonical caseless matching on case-insensitive volumes, including absent destinations and parent aliases; native UUID ownership compares canonical lowercase UUIDs. These keys govern submit, reservation, export and recovery while display spellings remain unchanged. Older raw lease spellings remain guards through alias-aware lookup, including multiple old holders. Resume and revive re-read conversation ownership inside the reservation transaction, after preparation and before leases or attempts.
- C-11.1, C-11.2 (2026-10-09, tier preferences): each task chain has one entry per tier, either a short model name or a non-empty list of distinct current model names in preference order. Routing flattens only the entries from the job's tier upward, removes duplicates keeping the first occurrence, and tries each model in order. Within a tier, a later model is tried only when no lane of an earlier model admits the job; order across tiers is unchanged. `fallback: upward-only` still excludes entries below the job's tier. Existing string-only policies retain their routing behavior; the shipped default chains are unchanged.
- C-6.9, C-6.10, C-6.11, C-6.3, C-11.4 (2026-10-08, defect D-1; incident 2026-10-03 18:36Z to 19:40Z, `docs/reports/2026-10-08-probe-vehicle-starvation.md`): a `hard` review drew four admission probes that ran to their 60 s deadline and read `unknown` or `transient`; each time it waited a minute and then a whole pass, 10 to 21 minutes, while later hard reviews probed the same Codex lanes and started. A probe says nothing about the job that carries it, so the jobs waiting on one now form a line in the pass's order and the first that could use a probe carries it (C-6.9); a job whose probe said nothing tries the model's other lanes before its first again (C-11.4); its clock runs from the probe's reservation, so a probe its deadline stopped leaves it due at once (C-6.10); `why` names the probe and whose turn it is (C-6.11); and a probe stopped at its deadline says so in its evidence (C-11.4).
- C-7.4 (2026-10-06, invariants audit A1): admission probe reservation and gate decisions re-read cancellation and terminal state inside their Store transactions; a refused gate contains the probe and releases its lease only after verified containment.
- C-6.16 (2026-10-06, Max: "give axiom promise maximum priority"): `admission.priority_callers` selects caller sessions case-insensitively, including their descendants. Their detached jobs sort ahead of session and background work, FIFO across tiers, and bypass the machine guard. `why` names the class and policy key; `status` names the configured callers from the registry when available. The default is null.

- PR #131 round three (2026-10-06), C-5.5, C-5.7: automatic resolution is exactly as safe as `kill --confirm-dead`, using the same full census, no weaker and no stricter. Verified-empty same-boot quarantines release within one pace; diagnostic boot observations never require a reboot. This superseded round two's blanket reboot requirement. Round four corrects the marker premise, bounds retained roots, adds cwd, and narrows the residual and legacy publication hold.

- PR #131 round two (2026-10-06), C-5.2, C-5.5, C-5.7: retained group members root the descendant walk. A private execution gate keeps a new provider from running until its launch identity is durably published. Proven boot changes discard old unqualified guardian, child and process-group roots. Writer boot observations are persisted independently of display reasons and survive restarts. Round three supersedes the original blanket reboot requirement; these observations are now diagnostic. Legacy missing child publication and corrupt recorded process-identity evidence remain conservative holds.

- PR #131 review fixes (2026-10-05), C-3.2, C-5.2, C-5.5, C-5.7: a live group member stays in the census even on a recycled recorded PID; every distinct recorded identity is retained and any match establishes ownership. The guardian republishes its launch receipt with the child PID and start identity before waiting or relaying, so unrelated child PID reuse can be excluded; legacy children without identities remain conservative holds. Quarantine text and non-object JSON are read as empty structured reasons; corrupt conversation manifests clear the notice pin. Durable retry-clock claims do not create audit rows, and an unchanged held census creates no duplicate event.

- C-5.7, C-5.5, C-5.11 (2026-10-05): quarantines resolve automatically once a fresh identity-aware census verifies every writer gone. The live store held 211 quarantined attempts, the oldest from 2026-09-22, with 167 `worktree:` and 209 `out:` leases plus native, native-session and conversation leases. A lost turn held `worktree:/Applications` for a 32-hour wait although recorded writer pid 23050 had exited about a day earlier (attempt `20261003-235153-turn-cv-1791085913864-9f4f22e40ef3/a1`, `writers remain after exit receipt`); claude-6 enrollment was fenced by a 9/26 `marker enumeration unavailable` quarantine; retention kept their worktrees while disk was short, and turn jobs wrote no caller notice. The hub's 10/5 `--confirm-dead` censuses were verified empty with identity checks and writable work was salvaged before release. The daemon now does that work itself on a durable 600-second pace, at most eight attempts per worker pass, announces dispatch salvage and turn releases, and lists older holds in status. Store schema 7 adds the indexed pace and a turn notification outbox; `default_policy.json` is unchanged.

- C-7.4 (2026-10-06, invariants audit A1): admission probe reservation and gate decisions re-read cancellation and terminal state inside their Store transactions; a refused gate contains the probe and releases its lease only after verified containment.
- C-11.9 (2026-10-06): human status and `status.json` show projected unused weekly quota per lane and scope from the provider's own readings, with an explicit unknown rate and provider totals. These forecasts are projections, not promises; admission does not use them.
- C-8.4 (2026-10-06, the 2.1.11 cut): with `SUBFLEET_RETENTION_DORMANT=1` in the daemon's environment, a retention pass prunes delivered service notices and nothing else. It selects, archives and deletes no job, marks the timer without an error, and logs once per daemon that retention is dormant. Any other value, or none, leaves retention as specified. 2.1.11's installer sets it in the launchd plist, not in `policy.json` (d574), because retention's fences still match a job's own tree exactly (design `docs/desktop/retention-archive.md` §15). The 2.1.12 install removes it once the fence fixes stacked on #134 land.
- C-8.4, C-13.4 (2026-10-05, shared folders on 2.1.11): port I5 to the retention-by-archive driver. Its pin census and archive commit recheck protect `folders.TURN` and `folders.READER` rows as `turn-folder`; its selecting transaction checks those rows again before taking the exclusive retirement fence. Admission checks that fence before reserving a writer or reader. The fence and archive journal use the same canonical spelling, established outside the store transaction. The archive, verification, byte-signature reclaim and registration-preservation rules remain binding. The historical shared-folder note about not-yet-started jobs is superseded by #76's `worktree-in-use` pin for recorded queued jobs.
- Review of the I5 port (599af189, 2026-10-05, P3-1 and P3-2; with the review of 31048e67, F1): C-8.4, a turn row on a folder inside a job's worktree, such as a conversation in a repository nested there, keeps the job in the pin census, the selecting transaction and the commit recheck; before, only a row on the worktree itself did, so once that turn's attempt was quarantined (its job `lost`, its row kept) nothing kept the tree. The commit also reads the rows on the journal's canonical spelling, as the selecting transaction does, so a recorded worktree in another case or an unrecorded one is kept too. The `turn-folder` pin now keeps only jobs with their own allocated worktree: an in-place job's folder is never removed, so holding those jobs kept only their rows and job directories, which no live turn needs, and stopped the turn and detached pools shrinking while a conversation was active. Turn reservation still fences only a turn's own folder (known limit, above).
- Review of 31048e67 (F3, 2026-10-05): C-8.4, a `worktree:` lease on a folder inside a job's own allocated worktree, held by anything but that job's retirement, keeps the job as `worktree-lease`, in the pin census (recorded spellings), the selecting transaction and the commit recheck (the canonical spelling), as a turn row there does. Before, `worktree-lease` matched the worktree itself only, and the selecting transaction checked only `worktree:<worktree>`, so once a detached in-place writer in a repository nested in the tree had its attempt quarantined (its job `lost`, its lease kept), nothing kept the job, and retention archived it and deleted its tree under the writer's folder. The leases are read by one key range (`worktree:<folder>/` up to `worktree:<folder>0`), never `LIKE`, which ignores ASCII case and reads `_` and `%` as wildcards. Writer reservation, like a turn's, still fences only the writer's own folder (known limit, above).

- C-24.5, C-24.4, C-6.3, C-6.5, C-6.6, C-6.11, C-8.4, C-13.4, C-26.9, C-26.14, C-29.10, C-29.11 (2026-09-29, shared folders): the owner ruled on 2026-09-28 that "nothing should be queued". Every conversation in the desktop store was in place, several in one folder (the Claude app's scratch folder among them), and every writable turn took `worktree:<folder>`, so a message to one conversation waited for another's turn in that folder: four waited between 29 minutes and 13 hours, while `why` said a lease was held and the app said "Waiting for capacity". Turns now share a folder, each with a row of its own (`worktree-turn:<target>:<job id>`, `worktree-read:<folder>:<job id>` for a read-only turn; `subfleet/folders.py`); `conversation:` and `native:` still keep one writer per conversation and per session. A detached writer still holds its folder alone, so a turn and a detached writer in one folder still exclude each other, and retention never reclaims a worktree a turn works in. C-24.4, C-6.11 and C-29.11: a waiting message always says why (I3), from admission's hold after every turn pass, naming the job and conversation a lease waits for, the first reset of closed lanes, and capacity only when it is. C-26.14: since a folder is shared, a turn's changes record which other conversations' turns wrote there while it ran (`turn_trees.shared_json`, found in the recording transaction whatever order starts and ends arrive in) and `turn.diff` and `conversation.diff` return them as `shared`, which the Changes pane names above the diff (C-29.10).
- Review of the shared-folder change (5e9f2fbd, 2026-09-29): C-29.11, the app reads a wait's kind from the start of its reason, not from any text in it, so a conversation titled with a kind's words ("fix the external-writer wait") no longer reads as a session open elsewhere. C-6.5, a folder is one target whatever case it is typed in (`folders.canonical`), for a detached writer's lease, a turn's rows (writable and read-only), a conversation's workspace and a handoff's alike; C-26.10 compares a workspace with the protected homes in that spelling too. C-26.14, a turn whose attempt ended with no end recorded is closed at the attempt's end, and the marks it no longer meets are dropped; C-24.5 and C-26.14 now say that a detached writer that released the folder between a turn's start snapshot and its reservation can leave edits in its diff unnamed; C-29.10 quotes at most 60 characters of a title. Retention still does not protect the folder of a turn job not yet started, and the brief's I5 wording (retention and salvage never act while any turn is live) is wider than the code's, which excludes writers only: both are known limits.
- Merge of release/217 into the shared-folder change (2026-10-01): C-24.4, a turn held `pin-unadmittable` (C-11.8: a Codex conversation's lane that can never take it) says so, naming the lane and its refusals; until then its message read "every lane that can take it is pin-unadmittable".
- C-4.5, C-6.14 (new), C-9.3 and C-23.44, with C-6.11, C-11.3, C-11.4 and C-15.1 (2026-10-03, for 2.1.9; incident 2026-09-30 15:43Z: an organisation disabled claude-5's Claude Code access, admission placed 37 jobs from about 20 sessions there within 40 s, and every one ended `failed` with rc 5 after one attempt; decision record `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-auth-dead-lane-fault.md`): an `auth-dead` attempt of a job with no lane pin, no conversation turn, that left its workspace as it found it is a lane fault, and the job moves on to the next candidate, once, without the attempt counting against `max_attempts`; a second `auth-dead` ends the job and leaves that lane enabled, so no job disables more lanes than before; `auth-dead` is read only from what the CLI and the provider wrote, never from a model's words; every classification records `model_answered`; and a lane no model has answered on for `admission.prove_idle_s` (900 s) takes one detached attempt, its pilot, until that attempt's stream shows its model answering, it ends, or `admission.prove_wait_s` (300 s) passes. A job that moved on is no pilot: on a lane not proven it waits for Subfleet's own probe (C-11.4), so a second dead lane is found by a probe, not by failing the job. The second half of each rule is from the independent reviews of PR #121.
- Review of the descriptor reconciliation (2026-10-04, of 3c8fe55, Opus and GPT reviews): C-15.5's last read when a `wait`'s deadline passes is back, and also follows a wake during the pass's own read (slicing the hub wait to look for a departed client had dropped it, so a job that ended just before the deadline was reported as a timeout); C-16.7 shuts a stream after a broken reply under its write lock, so no second reply joins the broken line; a failed `__init__` clears `stack_dumps` from `daemon.lock` before it lets SIGUSR1 go (C-3.6); the long-poll loops take busy as an empty poll, and a connect refused after busy as busy while the lock names a living daemon (a socket gone or a dead holder ends them), a retry inside `Client.call` refused at connect after busy reports busy by the same rule, malformed lines of every kind are answered exit 2, and a stream the daemon ended itself is not counted as a departure (these four as #55 on main); a request whose pool cannot start a thread no longer ends its reader, and `_end_stream` records only a connection still held; C-16.5 and C-16.7 bound queueing only when every held connection carries one request at a time, since one client that pipelines can delay every other, which is what the code gives; a failed `__init__`, or a `close()`, whose lock cannot be cleared keeps its SIGUSR1 handler and the stream it writes to, and while the interpreter runs no daemon in the process lets the signal fall back to its fatal default while any lock it holds says `stack_dumps`, holder of the signal or not, taking the signal and handing it on each under one lock so a close and a construction at the same moment cannot fall between each other's steps, and SIG_IGN is beneath the handler whenever faulthandler takes the signal fresh, on either thread, so what it puts back at the last close or at interpreter exit ends nothing, and a daemon that cannot make the signal safe does not say `stack_dumps` and never joins `_ADVERTISED`, and and an exception landing anywhere in a take, a close or a failed construction's cleanup cannot leave faulthandler holding a closed stream, and a member whose lock reads back without the flag leaves at the next settle once it has begun to leave or its object has been collected: every take and leave settles the registration to match the set (`_settle_sigusr1`), membership follows the lock read back, and a stream closes only when no hand-off can pick it and faulthandler cannot hold it (`_HELD_STREAMS`) (C-3.6, `_ADVERTISED`, `_DUMPS_LOCK`); a CLI wait loop with no `--timeout` takes a refused connect after busy with a lock that cannot say whether its holder lives as busy for at most 60 s; `wait` reads once more at its deadline even after a read on that pass, since the hub wakes a waiter only after its own read.

- C-29.13 integration (2026-10-01): inline question and approval cards and request sheets use the same reading font sizes and persisted text scale as the conversation; their rewrite had restored fixed system captions and callouts.

- 2.1.10 integration (2026-10-01): merging the inline question and approval cards (C-27.1, C-27.2) with approvals within reach (C-27.5). Views carry the provider's request id under both names; C-27.1 permits only an exact join. A view from a daemon older than both fields gets an independent card keyed by immutable approval id, so stale question drafts cannot answer a replacement request. The strip's Review brings a pending question into view, where its card is the form, and opens the request sheet only for a tool request.
- C-12.9 (2026-09-30, d714; ships in 2.1.10): writable detached Claude jobs start with no MCP servers, using `--strict-mcp-config` and an empty config; repeatable `run --mcp NAME` opts one job into exactly the named servers. Submission resolves and records the entries, retries and resumes keep them, and no policy default enables any server. Read-only and isolated reviews keep their existing empty MCP configuration; Subfleet.app conversation turns are outside this ruling and keep their existing launch behavior.
- C-11.8, with C-4.1, C-6.9, C-6.11, C-6.12, C-11.2 and C-17.3 (2026-09-30; incident 2026-09-29 ~21:53Z, a job pinned `-a claude-9` stayed queued with no word after claude-9 became the desktop login, and held 38 younger Opus jobs): a job pinned to a lane that can never admit it (the desktop login while Claude Code uses it, a lane disabled, owned by v1, holding another account or with a latched credential, one closed further out than `admission.pin_hold_far_s`, one its own exclusions name, or no such lane) is held `pin-unadmittable` on every pass and holds nobody back; its caller's session gets one notice naming the lane, the reason and the fix; and after `admission.pin_grace_s` (30 minutes) with the lane still so it fails with rc 3. A job with no pin that every lane refuses for such a reason holds nobody back either. The notice waits until the state has lasted 60 s, names its job for `notice.pending`, goes once per job, ever, and is withdrawn unread when it stops being true; an expired token is standing only on a Codex lane whose one heal for the credential epoch has run and left it expired (a Claude home lane's heal is retried every 20 minutes); `notice.mark` now marks a service notice (C-15.3). Design call delegated to Claude (Max, 2026-09-24): fail rather than only notify, because a notice that is not a job's end reaches a session only at its next prompt, while an agent blocked in `subfleet wait` wakes only on a terminal state, and a job that never started has no `max_wall_s` to end it.
- C-8.4, C-13.4 (2026-10-01, the four notes #81 left on #76; design `docs/desktop/retention-archive.md` revision 5): a file is matched on its ctime too, and one that had other links when archived whose ctime alone moved is deleted only if its bytes still hash to the archived sha256 (a same-size write whose mtime was put back was deleted). A job whose tree another tool holds aside (`<anything>.<name>` beside it, as `disk-guard` names it) is kept, and so is one whose tree left or came back while it was being retired. The tree admission allocated before `jobs.worktree` recorded it (`worktrees/<job id>`) is the job's own allocated worktree. A job whose tree and workdir are both gone finds its registration and repository through the repositories the store's jobs are in; a salvage ref whose repository cannot be found says so.
- C-8.4, C-13.4, C-5.1 (2026-09-30, final review of e50716e8; design `docs/desktop/retention-archive.md` revision 4): inside a regenerable directory's tool entries only what a rule proves regenerable is deleted without a copy (a file an installed distribution's RECORD lists with a matching sha256, from a distribution uv installed from an index or a network URL; bytecode with a magic number beside its source; a tool cache's own file names), and everything else there, all of `node_modules` included, is archived; git's names and the disk's are compared NFC-normalized and casefolded. Every report of freed bytes also reports `added_bytes`, what the archive adds (bundle, manifest, summary, rows, byte copies), and the log lines the net. A job whose baseline no network remote holds (a host on this machine is no network remote) and whose bundle would carry more than `retention.remote_less_history_bytes` (default 64 MiB) of history is kept. A job registered in a repository inside another job's tree pins that job while it has rows, and is kept while that tree is not there. An admin directory reduced to `index` and `logs/` is no registration; its bytes are archived. A published archive keeps no progress log. Retention's children run under the `utility` QoS clamp and its in-process steps with lowered thread I/O.
- C-8.4, C-13.4, C-17.1 (2026-09-29, d635 round 2; design `docs/desktop/retention-archive.md` revision 3, review of a9a6cbf4): regenerable output is deleted with a retired job's tree, not archived: in a directory whose structure says it is a virtualenv (`pyvenv.cfg` with `home`), `node_modules` (beside a `package.json`), `__pycache__` (only `.pyc`/`.pyo`) or a tool cache (`.pytest_cache`, `.ruff_cache`, `.mypy_cache`, `.uv-cache`, `.tox` with a `CACHEDIR.TAG`), which git tracks nothing in and ignores entirely and which no nested repository holds, the top-level entries that tool writes (all of a `__pycache__`), unless one holds a `.git`; anything else there is archived, as live trees held agents' logs, scripts, a bundle and a bare repository inside tagged caches; `build/`, `dist/`, `target/` and `.cache/` stay archived. Events, each pool in a pass's result, and log lines report the bytes freed apart from the bytes moved into the archive. A job's salvage commits are parents of its anchor, the bundle's only head, so a salvage commit a network remote already holds no longer wedges its job. A job whose size cannot be measured is decided, not waiting, and the daemon's catch-up backs off while passes change nothing; an error keeps the archive cache and backs off; a slice always moves forward, and parked jobs take turns. The conversation service rebuilds a worktree without a repository-wide prune. `retention survey --sample N` estimates what retirement frees.
- C-8.4, C-13.4, C-17.1 (2026-09-29, Max's d635 ruling; design `docs/desktop/retention-archive.md` revision 2): retention archives before it deletes. Everything of a finished job that exists nowhere else (every file of its allocated worktree and job directory, the worktree's admin directory and every object it names, its salvage commits, its rows) goes into an archive retention owns and is read back; only then are the rows deleted, and only entries whose signature is unchanged right before their unlink are deleted, anything else going to `retention-conflicts/`. A salvage ref in the verified archive bundle is referenced elsewhere. A pass retires a bounded batch oldest first without sizing everything first, parks a slow job with its progress and defers busy ones. New verbs `retention archives | restore | survey`.
- C-23.28 (2026-09-27, six-hour flag-sync starvation): a full mirror pass services due flag-only hot passes at checkpoints under its existing writer lock, preserves independent pass health and unknown-copy holds, and refreshes stale full flag decisions; cold inventory reads reuse descriptor metadata and warm sweeps use DirEntry.stat, with GIL-contended measurements in `docs/reports/2026-09-27-mirror-inventory-contention.json`.
- Review of those fixes (7da13417, 2026-09-27): C-26.6 says an interrupt is not new provider work, so a replay after a recorded outcome still sends the driver's interrupt (a model mismatch's) and withholds only the user frame and approval answers; C-23.14 and C-25.5 keep a scrubbed head and tail of a text over the scrubber's budget rather than omitting it whole, resolving the blocks that span lines over the whole text first, scrub the assembled brief whole, and classify a tool input of any size for credential reads in linear time; C-23.36 caps the brief's sections at 245,760 characters in all; C-23.35 treats a skipped desktop record, or a store directory that cannot be listed, as unknown ownership, which revive refuses as it does an owned session and names.
- Conversation 2.1.9 review fixes (2026-09-27): C-24.3 verifies accepted text on read; C-25.3 bounds and refuses symlink/FIFO admission manifests; C-23.35 skips unsafe desktop metadata records; C-26.6 and C-30.4 preserve saved execution boundaries and defer legacy stops until replay catches up; C-28.1 verifies final image bytes by digest and snapshots Codex images; C-25.3 and C-27.1 use no-follow state reads with approval digest verification; C-23.14 and C-25.5 make redaction linear and cap its input before matching.
- Steer (2026-09-28, Max): C-24.9 delivers a queued person message into its running turn, with durable binding, cancellation before handover, per-message fate settlement and idempotent replay. C-24.5/C-24.6/C-24.7, C-25.2, C-26.5/C-26.6 and C-29.7 carry the new states and `steer.v1` interface. Provider evidence and remaining live checks are in `docs/steer-provider-claims.md`.
- Steer, after its review (2026-09-28): C-24.9 and C-29.7 state what shipped (DESIGN.md sections 8 and 9): any queued person message may steer unless a repair message is queued, Return steers and ⌘Return queues for later, `/` and `!` input never steers (the daemon refuses it too), the read states, and Esc and Stop. C-24.5: a missed steer runs next, a deferral of its turn included. C-24.7: interrupting a steering message stops its host, and only a turn with steers asks Claude for `cancel_queued`. C-24.9: a turn with no steers is checked against a recording of the pre-steer code. C-25.2 lists `message.steer` and its optional `into`, which refuses a late steer once another turn runs. C-26.5: the Claude watchdog never runs during a steer’s own turn and gives up only the steers its round named; eof keeps a held result. C-26.6: a steer’s images resolve by digest, and no steer failure ends its host’s runner; a Codex turn drops another turn’s notifications only once its own id is known. C-29.7: a `not-steerable` answer is asked again briefly. Steer, second pass (2026-09-29): C-26.5, a steer’s own turn runs until a result or stdout’s end even once a stop calls it cancelled, a result ends every turn a started steer is part of, and a result held only for a steer never written ends at once; C-24.5, a rolled-back handoff keeps a missed steer’s mark; C-24.9 and C-25.2, a live turn with no runner yet refuses `not-steerable`; C-24.9, invariant 5’s recording covers every history the clause names and pins the one deliberate difference, and invariant 1 also runs through the runner’s loop; C-29.7, Esc passes over a too-late steer while the app still sees it where it was, and Stop interrupts only a message read as its own turn’s.

- Sections 24 to 30 (C-24.1 to C-30.4, revised after the 2026-09-24 contract review) restore the desktop workspace on v2 (Max, 2026-09-24; `plan.md` "Desktop workspace restored, 2026-09-24"; specification `docs/desktop/design.md`). They supersede the cockpit half of `plan-b-rev4.md:387` and the words "and cockpit branch" at `:403`; the Traycer exclusion stands.
- C-2.2 adds `conversations/`, `attachments/`, `catalog.json` and `run/`. C-3.4 names the desktop app as a client that mutates only through daemon ops. C-16.2 lists every op the daemon serves, including the ops added since version 2 and the conversation ops of section 25. C-20.1 adds `tests/frontend/`. C-23.54 is unchanged: a conversation turn is a submission (C-26.1).
- After the first end-to-end runs (2026-09-24): C-24.7 orders the stop escalation as the runner and IR-3 do (SIGINT before closing stdin); C-24.8 and C-26.7 send repair messages ahead of queued person messages; C-25.6 lets a development daemon accept its development build and identifies the app by its executable path; C-9.3, C-11.4 and C-15.1 name the turn exceptions the code already makes; C-26.8 names how a turn's account and model are checked, after a probe of Claude Code 2.1.280 showed `initialize` reports an email (lanes keep uuids as `identity`) and a catalog with no bare `opus` or `fable` value.
- C-25.2 (2026-09-24, from the installed app): receipts from `conversation.open` and `message.status` carry the person's text.
- C-26.9 (2026-09-24, after the install): turns have their own capacity pool (`conversations.max_active_turns`, `conversations.turn_slots_per_lane`), so detached work that fills every lane slot does not hold an attended turn.
- C-26.9 and C-6.9 (2026-09-27, Max): the two turn caps default to null, which is no cap. With neither cap set, a turn waits only when no lane can take it (a closure, a disabled lane, the desktop login, a probe it would need) or a lease it needs is held (its conversation's, its native session's, or a writable checkout's), never for a count of other turns and never `behind-older-job` behind another turn, because an uncapped pool has no slot a later turn could take from an older one. A policy may still set either cap to a positive whole number, and then the turn pool keeps its FIFO as before. Turns also keep FIFO on a lease, capped or not: a turn held waiting for a lease (a writable checkout, a conversation, a native session) is queued for it, and no later turn of the pass takes it, even when the holder released it after the pass began; the later turn is held `lease-held` with the free lease under `queued` (not `leases`, which lists only leases another job holds) and `queued_behind` naming the older one, and `why` says the lease is kept for an older turn (reviews of 1b38d641 and 8f14a50f: a lease freed mid-pass went to whichever turn looked next). Between two turns only a writable checkout can in practice be contested: one conversation has one live turn and its own native session. Detached jobs cannot share an output path or a writable checkout (submit refuses the second), and two detached resumes of one session are pinned to one lane and model, so C-6.9 already orders them: the queue is the turn pool's. Incident: a conversation's turn waited 12 minutes with "Waiting for capacity" because the only two open Claude lanes each ran another conversation's turn under the old default of one turn per lane (three across the fleet).
- C-6.4, C-6.5, C-6.9, C-6.11, C-6.13 (new), C-10.3, C-11.2, C-11.3, C-11.4, C-18.1 and C-26.9 (2026-09-27, Max: "we should uncap everything and instead use prioritization"; plan `docs/decisions/2026-09-27-uncap-and-prioritize.md`): the four concurrency caps (`max_active_attempts`, `max_in_flight_per_lane`, `max_in_flight_unmeasured` and the fixed 1 beneath it, and `max_active_attempts_per_parent`, until then a hidden 1) are null by default, which is no cap; jobs are placed by priority (attended turns, then jobs someone is waiting on, then background work; tier and age within each), and C-6.9's hold-back applies only in a pool with a count cap; lanes fill in load bands (`admission.lane_spread`) so no single lane takes every job; the desktop login's lane is refused only while Claude Code uses that login (Max: excluding it "makes sense if we're using sf thru the cc app but not if thru the sf app") and otherwise sorts last; a machine guard (C-6.13), off by default, can hold background and session jobs, never turns, at the door while the machine is saturated; the submit counts `max_writable_per_session` and `max_child_jobs` are null by default too (2026-09-28, Max: "remove *all* caps"); no attempt holds a lane's probe lease (`slot:0`), so a probe waits for no job, and an admission probe is not started on a credential latched since its evaluation (C-11.4); and a probe cycle releases every timer hold it took (C-18.1). Revised after the Astra plan gate's round 1: the desktop signal validates each row's process, counts headless runs Subfleet does not own, treats what it cannot read as use, and is read again inside the reservation. Incident: at 20:26 EDT that day 35 jobs were pending with none placed for 2,175 s and no lane open: four Claude lanes each at `max_in_flight_per_lane` 2, six Codex lanes each at the unmeasured 1 that no policy could raise, and 34 jobs `behind-older-job` behind one that had no slot.
- C-26.13 (2026-09-24, conversation sessions and the sessions kit): `subfleet hook SessionStart` and `UserPromptSubmit` do nothing inside a process Subfleet launched, detected by the C-5.1 markers, after a probe of Claude Code 2.1.280 showed its command hooks run with them (`docs/desktop/reviews/2026-09-24-live-probes.md`); C-15.2 and C-23.34 name the exception. The `sessions` op reports `conversation_sessions` and the sessions kit never lists, nudges, revives, cold-sweeps or hands off one, because a conversation's transcript gains one SDK prompt per turn and stops looking like a lane run (C-23.31) after two; C-26.3 now says "listed" and "handed off" too. The daemon refuses a `resume` of a turn or of a conversation's session and a `revive` of one, at submit and again at admission, rather than letting the `native:` lease make them take turns with the conversation.
- C-26.13 and C-15.2 narrowed (2026-09-24, review of the change above): inside a process Subfleet launched, `SessionStart` and `UserPromptSubmit` still surface and mark the notices that name a job, because a job a conversation turn dispatched that ends between turns has no other layer to reach the conversation (layer 2 waits only on running jobs); only notices that name no job (`ping`, nudges, timer alerts, imported continuations) are withheld there, and `SessionStart` still wakes nothing. Tested end to end with a fake turn that runs `subfleet run` as its Bash tool.
- C-26.13 exit codes (2026-09-24, review): `sessions continue --scope cold`, which v1's `subfleet revive <id>` reaches, exited 0 when every session it named was a conversation's or a lane run, where the other scopes exit 7; it now exits 7 with the reason and the fix, for revive and handoff recoveries alike, as does `sessions revive --dry-run` for such a session. A cold handoff re-checks each session against the daemon's lists and holds only the one refused. The clause now says which requests exit 7.
- C-26.13 and an older daemon (2026-09-24, review): between a code update and a daemon restart the kit met a daemon that ran conversations but answered `sessions state` without `conversation_sessions`, read that as "none", and `sessions revive --revive --force` submitted a revive of a conversation's session. A reply without the list is now a version signal: exit 69 and the restart fix (`registry.conversation_ids_of`).
- C-26.13 and C-6.2 (2026-09-24, review): `submit` refused a resume or revive of a conversation's session before it looked up the request id, so a retry of one accepted before a person opened the session in the app got exit 7 and no job id for a job that existed. A request id a job already holds is now answered as C-6.2 says, and admission still fails that job.
- C-25.4 (2026-09-24, review IR-6): `reset` is exact. The floor is now the highest event sequence number compaction removed, `reset` is true exactly when the cursor is below it (a cursor of 0 included), and each page carries the floor.
- C-8.4, C-26.12 (2026-09-24, review IR-17): detached and turn jobs are retained under separate policy budgets (`retention.*`), the conversation service pins the turn jobs it still needs, and a notice with no session pins nothing. C-25.4 says when compaction runs (`conversations.compact_after_s`, `conversations.compact_per_tick`). C-30.1 (design D-23): the conversation tick starts a catalog run every `conversations.catalog_interval_s` without waiting for it, and `conversation.list` reports a missing, damaged or old catalog.
- C-26.1 (2026-09-24): a turn submit refused before its job exists fails the message `not-delivered: <reason>` when the refusal is of the message as it is (exit 2 or 7), and otherwise leaves it waiting with `deferred: <why>` and a backoff; a message withdrawn while its job is submitted takes the job with it.
- C-25.3, C-26.10 (2026-09-24, design D-16): a worktree conversation's worktree (path, branch, source, repository, base) is recorded through the store's update and returned; repeating the create finishes an interrupted cut, and until then the conversation neither accepts nor dispatches messages; `conversation.create` runs on the file pool.
- C-26.7 (2026-09-24): a limited message's continuation is written before the message shows `failed`, one per limited message.
- Review IR-18 and IR-34 (2026-09-24): C-18.2 rows carry `kind` and its `live`, `recent` and `counts` leave turn jobs out; C-29.6 names the Claude `windows` list keyed by scope and window, `claude.earliest_reset`, and the `conversations` section read through a read-only connection on the timer thread.
- Review IR-19 (2026-09-24): C-26.12 keeps turn jobs out of `list`, and so out of `runs`, `wait --mine` and `wait --last`, unless asked; C-17.1's `runs` gains `--kind K` and `--include-turns`.
- Review of the IR-18, IR-19 and IR-34 work (2026-09-24): C-26.12 says what `runs` does with a daemon that does not advertise `jobs.kind.v1` (C-25.1): it sends no `kind` or `include_turns` field, refuses `--kind` with exit 69, and drops turn rows itself.
- Same review: C-29.6's `claude.earliest_reset` also leaves out a lane whose identity is mismatched (C-10.6), which stays enabled and keeps its last readings but is never a candidate.
- Same review: C-29.6's `conversations` section names what "could not be read" covers (an OS error such as a root the reader may not search, not only a SQLite one) and that a failed reader never stops `status.json` being written.
- Reconciliation (2026-09-24, design D-14, reviews IR-5, IR-7, IR-23): C-24.6 names the evidence, applies it to every turn without a terminal event (the driver's refusals before sending included) and bounds re-admission; C-24.7 adds the withdrawal tombstone; C-24.8 blocks on delivery, not only acknowledgement, and after a Claude message is resolved `delivered`; C-24.1 records a Claude session id only once the session exists.
- The relay handshake (2026-09-24, review IR-27): C-26.4 adds the advertised frame cap, the `status` request a runner makes before sending or replaying, and bounded retries.
- The turn clocks become policy (2026-09-24): C-24.7 names `conversations.stop_sigint_after_s`, `stop_close_after_s` and `stop_contain_after_s` and the order the loader enforces; C-26.5 names `conversations.after_result_s`; C-26.9 names `conversations.approval_wait_s`.
- Design revision 3 (2026-09-24, review of the first implementation): design D-13 orders SIGINT before closing stdin, as C-24.7 already did, and D-14 states the no-session case C-24.6 already named; section 24 cites revision 3.
- D-25 built (2026-09-24): new C-26.14 (numbered after the sessions-kit rule C-26.13; a turn's changes: start and end snapshots, `turn.diff`, `conversation.diff`); C-25.2 names every conversation op, now with the two diff ops, and says an added op is a capability, not a new schema (it named protocol dataclasses the conversation ops never had); C-25.3 lets the two diff ops run git, on the file pool; C-26.10 also forbids a salvage ref when a quarantined turn is released and points at C-26.14. The conversation store is schema 2.
- After the review of the D-25 build (2026-09-24): C-16.2 takes the section 25 ops' shapes from design §5, not from protocol dataclasses (as C-25.2 already did); C-25.1 says `conversation_schema` (1) versions the ops' shapes, not the store; C-26.14 counts the end snapshot's tries (three in one daemon run, one at a quarantine's release) instead of calling them retries, takes a message's latest attempt as the one recorded last, names the `conversation.watch` row an end writes, says a cut listing also sets `files_truncated`, lets `path` name a directory, removes key material a hunk's edge, the cut or a hunk header separates from its armour lines, scrubs each diff line without its `+`, `-` or space prefix, bounds the text returned (after decoding and scrubbing) rather than git's output, and adds the reason `workspace-gone`; C-25.3 names `conversation.create` as the other op that runs git (a worktree conversation's cut, C-26.10), which the D-25 line above left out, and moves it to the file pool.
- C-30.4 is rewritten with the legacy cockpit import (2026-09-24): the v1 outbox is the cockpit's message outbox, so its notice mapping is replaced by per-message classification into `legacy` conversation history, the `cockpit` row changes from drop to a reported, retained journal, and both rows move to milestone 9. The earlier text let a message proven not dispatched move through an id mapping; this release moves none (every non-terminal message and journal entry, with its session, keeps its legacy owner), and names the report, the dry run and the lock.
- C-24.7 adds the dispatcher's claim on a queued message (2026-09-24, found while building review IR-28): `message.cancel` checked that a queued message had no job and then marked it cancelled, while the dispatcher could create the job in between, so a withdrawn message could still be sent.
- Labelled handoffs (2026-09-24, design D-18, review IR-28): C-30.3 names the `conversation.handoff` op, its brief readers (Claude transcripts and, new, Codex rollouts), how pending messages move and when a handoff is refused; its owner layer becomes `fake`, where its end-to-end tests run.
- Merge of the D-25 branch (2026-09-25): C-25.2 lists `conversation.runs` with the diff ops and keeps the receipts' `text`; `conversation.runs` is advertised as `runs.v1`, as C-25.2 says an added op is; C-25.3 keeps the worktree create's two writes; C-26.10 keeps the worktree conversation and points its snapshots at C-26.14.
- Review of the pre-install fixes (2026-09-25): C-6.6 says where a job in an allocated worktree starts (the caller's place for Claude and its resumes, the top for Codex, whose sandbox writes only under its working directory, and the top when the caller's head lacks the directory); C-6.7 keeps the workspace note under `--no-preamble` and records `preamble` for resumes; C-29.8 says how a history page ends and hands on its cursor. A second review of those fixes: C-6.6 names the caller's place as git spells it and resumes from where the source attempt's launch started; C-29.8 states the read bound, that the page reaching the file's first row ends the history, and that the app follows pages that add nothing.
- Review of the legacy import (2026-09-25): C-30.4 holds a session for any journal entry whatever the outbox says of its id, holds every session when the journal cannot be read, and blocks `legacy-owner` a conversation an earlier pass bound while its session is held again, lifting that block once the session is settled.
- The daemon's descriptors (2026-09-25): C-16.1 raises the open-file limit, caps and answers connections past 512, and keeps `accept` serving through a descriptor or memory shortage; it had exited on EMFILE under launchd's limit of 256, and 32 readers had left new requests waiting out the client's 15 s.
- Review of the descriptor hotfix (2026-09-25): C-16.1's cap counts only connections still read. `wait`s whose clients had left had held every place, so every client could be refused. The daemon now drops what a departed client sent that no pool has started, answers busy when no reader thread can start, and counts and reports busy answers in `daemon.status`. It listens with the system's largest queue, and C-16.1 says a failed `accept` loses its connection. C-15.4 counts a `wait`'s deadline from when the daemon read it. The CLI's wait loops and the gate poll ask again after a busy answer, and `daemon status` reports a busy daemon as running. C-29.2: the app shows a busy daemon as busy, keeps a send answered busy queued, and checks availability again once the feed answers.
- Descriptor follow-up verification (2026-09-25): C-16.1 keeps a queued reader from handling a request until thread creation succeeds, so an exit-69 refusal still guarantees that nothing ran. CLI waits and gate polls bound every retry transport timeout to the original remaining budget, and never begin another poll after backoff reaches the deadline. The connection, cancellation, deadline, status and accept-recovery regressions also run with socket pairs when a sandbox denies listener binding.
- C-30.3 makes handoff cancellation recoverable (2026-09-25, design D-18, reviews IR-2 and IR-28): prepare before fencing, commit the move and fence lift together, and restore cancelled messages in order on failure or the first control-loop pass after restart.
- Merge of the handoff branch (2026-09-25): C-24.7's claim covers a waiting message too and no longer blocks `message.cancel`, which withdraws any message with no job bound (the job the dispatcher then creates is cancelled before any attempt); C-24.6's readmissions are counted by the message's turn jobs a provider reached, so a handoff's rolled-back cancels do not use them up; C-30.3's handoff moves a waiting message with no job under the store's guard, and checks a moved attachment's file before cancelling anything (review of 6290a51).
- Review of the catalog-close fix, #47 (2026-09-26): C-30.1 now says when a catalog run may write, its exit statuses when it declines (3; 4 for a fence that is not a pipe) and that the service logs a declined run it still tracks; a declined run had exited 0, so a false decline left the catalog stale without a word. The fence sits above descriptor 2: in a daemon with stdin closed it had been fd 0, which the run's /dev/null stdin replaced. C-25.3: `close()` finishes the file ops already running and runs none after, and a file op never makes the state root; an `attachment.add` (or a worktree create) that finished after `close()` had made the removed root again.
- Reviews of the catalog-close follow-ups, 39223c9 (2026-09-26): C-25.3 says the conversation service opens a native session's files only as regular files and never blocks in `open()`. `close()` had begun to wait for the file ops already running, and one handed a FIFO where a Codex rollout belonged (`conversation.history`, a handoff) blocked in `open()` until a writer came, holding `close()` with it. C-30.1 now says a run checks before each file it publishes rather than that it writes only while its service is open: it publishes by path, so a root removed or replaced after a check can fail the write (exit 1) or receive it; and the service names a reason only for exits 3 and 4.
- Turn runners after `close()` (2026-09-26, from the review of #47): C-25.3 now says `close()` waits, at most 5 s in all, for each turn runner to finish the iteration it is in, adopts none once it has begun, and leaves a conversation store that refuses every read and write (`store-closed`); C-24.3 says a message's text, like an approval's request and the model catalog, is written only while the store is open and never creates the state root. `close()` had only asked the runners to stop. A runner still in its iteration when `close()` returned made a removed state root again (`conversations/models.json` from the catalog in a turn's initialize answer, `conversations/<id>/approvals/` from a permission request), and its last flush raised out of its thread, which then never set `finished`. A runner the control loop was adopting while `close()` ran could start after `close()` had stopped the others, with no one to stop it.
- The mirror's reads (2026-09-26, the gap the reviews of 39223c9 left): C-23.28 says the mirror opens what it reads outside its state root only as regular files and never blocks in `open()`. The daemon runs the mirror on the timers' own worker, and `Daemon.close()` waits for it through `Timers.stop()`; a FIFO named like a record in the desktop store (which is listed by name), in the app log's place, at `cc-mirror.json` or where a transcript belongs blocked a pass in `open()` until a writer came, and `close()` with it. A FIFO, device or directory where a record belongs is in no sidebar and holds nothing, as a directory already was; one where a dead session's transcript belongs is no transcript, where revival had taken it for another writer's and spread the session.
- C-28.1 (2026-09-26, found while fixing the review of #47): each `attachment.add` copies through a temporary file of its own. Two adds of the same bytes at once (the app resending after its 15 s timeout while the first add still ran, or two clients; an add still queued for the file pool when the app gave up is dropped with its connection) shared `.<sha256>.<pid>.tmp`: the second open truncated the first's file and at least one add failed, in 40 of 40 trials, sometimes both with no row registered, though the stored copy was right. A copy that fails now removes its temporary file where the directory lets it; a temporary name already taken is drawn again; a short write goes on until the copy is whole, and a write that makes no progress fails the copy instead of looping.
- C-28.1 (2026-09-26, found while fixing the entry above): a changed stored copy is repaired, and its name is on disk before its row. `attachment.add` copied only when no file had the copy's name and then refused a hash mismatch, so once `attachments/<sha256>.<ext>` held other bytes (disk damage, a stray write) every re-add failed with `copy-mismatch` and the driver failed every message naming it `attachment-missing`, for good; a copy it could not read failed every re-add with an unhandled error, and a symlink to the same bytes was kept. It now copies whenever the name does not hold a regular file with those bytes. It also never fsynced `attachments/` after the rename, which C-8.1 requires of a published file and `store._publish` does for message text, so a crash could keep the committed row and lose the copy's name. The directory is now fsynced before every row, also by an add that finds the copy in place, which may find it before the add that renamed it has synced.
- C-28.1 (2026-09-27, review of 1808f61): the driver's check judges a stored copy as an add does. It read the copy with `read_bytes()`, which followed a symlink and blocked on a FIFO at the copy's name for good, holding the conversation tick that builds turns and every tick after it (C-25.3's regular-file, never-blocking rule, now for attachments). The daemon's own copy is also of the row's size, read at most one byte past it, owned by its user, with no second link and no group or other mode bits: a re-add had kept a copy made readable by others or hard-linked from outside, and hashed a stray file of any size in full. A re-add also records the copy's path, so a moved state root no longer leaves a row naming the old one.
- Review of 39223c9, finding 4 (2026-09-26): C-5.1 now says how a guardian's launch gate works and that it sits above descriptor 2, as C-30.1's fence does. The daemon took each gate (an attempt's, a probe's, a Claude lane re-enrolment's) from `os.pipe()`, which returns the lowest free descriptors: in a daemon whose fd 0, 1 or 2 had been closed after it started, the read end took that number, the guardian's /dev/null standard stream replaced it, and the guardian read end-of-file after the daemon had written the byte and exited 127 without starting the provider. The gates and the fence now come from one allocator, `procs.pipe_above_stdio`.
- Review of the round-3 fixes (2026-09-25): C-29.8's read bound is up to 68 MiB (the cap plus 64 MiB), blank space longer than the probe ends the history, and opening a conversation follows no empty pages; C-6.6's caller's place accepts a committed name that starts with dots (`..data`); the scrubber counts every value a header held and removes a credential given as a flag's separate argument or holding an escaped quote.
- Review of the store-contention fixes (2026-09-25, of 5841d8b): C-3.6 `daemon stacks` signals only a daemon whose `daemon.lock` says `stack_dumps`, so a daemon without the handler is refused, not killed; C-3.7 refuses a transaction inside a snapshot on its thread, finds a probe record by index, filters no NaN payload in SQL, keeps two read connections from snapshots, bounds and logs every wait for one, builds views outside their snapshot, closes a read connection only after its read, and bounds the write-ahead log. C-15.5 states the wait latency the code gives, and its hub restarts, never leaving a waiter asleep. C-3.6 drops a report that raises. C-6.3 reserves on an early evaluation only while its readings are fresh, and names the two changes the generation does not count.
- Review of those fixes (2026-09-26, of b3d463d): C-6.3 reserves on an early evaluation only before its horizon (`capacity.decision_horizon`), which adds a closure's end and a confirmed override's end to a reading's freshness, since either can close a lane, and one view decides each override at its own clock; C-3.6 lists the `read-pool` and `read-hold` reports, says a lock that changes between `daemon stacks`' two reads exits 1, and lets a second daemon in a process take the SIGUSR1 registration over; C-3.7 refuses a read still opening a connection when the store closes, guards `json_extract` with `CASE`, and names the one payload a rewrite reads differently (a repeated key, which no writer makes, and which also hides its first value's older probe records); C-15.5 says what the wait hub does when it cannot start a thread, and `daemon.status` reports it as `wait_hub`.
- Second review of the legacy import (2026-09-25): the import's hold is the conversation's own `legacy_hold` column, beside the service's `blocked_by`, and keeps every turn off the conversation, so C-24.5 defines a blocked conversation by either column and C-6.11 adds the `conversation-blocked` and `message-settled` holds. C-30.4 also holds sessions the cockpit is using now (every session while its broker runs, a session with a live worker), keeps history out of a session a live Claude process outside Subfleet is in, holds every session while the outbox or the workers file cannot be read, holds every conversation of a held session whatever its origin, and records the held sessions so one opened later is bound held; it fences before placing any history, keys a session in one spelling, never reads a missing, empty or v2 directory as the v1 state, adds a recorded `--cockpit-retired`, and says which dispositions a later pass resolves. C-26.3 says what is enforced: `resume` refuses a turn source and a conversation-bound session, the kit and the daemon never nudge or revive one, and a live Claude writer is a wait. C-24.7 lets a person withdraw a message waiting to be re-admitted, records a cancel's stop before it withdraws, and never writes a message whose stop came before it was handed over. C-30.2 names a moved session's workspace.
- Merge of the legacy import's review fixes into the desktop line (2026-09-26): the hold stays its own column (C-24.5), but the line's own designs decide what the two built twice: a withdrawal against the dispatcher is C-24.7's claim, and the cancel's stop, recorded first, keeps a runner admission started meanwhile from writing the message; a Claude external writer is checked at dispatch and at launch (C-26.3), not at admission, so C-6.11 has no `external-writer` hold; the sessions kit's fence is C-26.13's, which reads a daemon that does not list conversation sessions as too old rather than as none bound. A turn the hold withheld is re-admitted by `reconcile.settle` (`readmit:legacy-owner`, a wait, like another writer), and a view and `status.json` show a held conversation as blocked `legacy-owner`.
- Review of that merge (2026-09-26, of 3c1a34e): C-26.3 finds an outside Claude writer whichever case the conversation's binding and the registry spell the session in, at dispatch and at launch, and calls it a dispatch and launch wait, not an admission one; C-26.13's fences, the kit's and the daemon's, compare a UUID in either case on both sides, and the daemon refuses to record a nudge of a session only a turn attempt names; C-24.7 records a person's stop, and a runner hands the message frame over, under one lock per message, so a stop recorded after the runner started still keeps the message unwritten, records a cancel's stop in the transaction that withdraws, so a refused cancel stops nothing, and names the unbound exception to its opening sentence; C-24.6's three re-admissions are not used up by waits for the session's other writer; C-30.4 reads a journal entry without a request, or with a message id or image paths of another shape, as an unreadable journal, and reports a row whose image paths or service tier are malformed `unreadable-row` before writing anything; C-30.2 names the latest matching cwd, and the catalog shows a moved session once, by its newest copy, and judges it by that cwd. After an independent review of those fixes: a person's stops and cancels are serialized per message rather than daemon-wide, a lone surrogate in the journal, the workers file or a row's payload holds or reports rather than ending the pass, and the kit's refusal compares the named sessions in either case.
- Admission latency (2026-09-26, after a conversation turn waited about 725 s to be placed): C-6.3's reserving transaction evaluates no route and builds no view; it checks the early decision on a few rows read by index, judges again only the lanes whose rows changed, and goes on with exactly the decision an evaluation would make whenever those lanes decide it; otherwise it evaluates again off the lock, up to `ROUTE_TRIES` times, then keeps the job's place (C-6.11 `route-moved`). `ROUTE_REUSE_S` is gone. C-26.9: turns have an admission pass of their own, run first and also beside the detached pass.
- Reviews of the close() stack and the mirror's reads (2026-09-26, of 9ff0c1b, 4d3d3ea and 8172685): C-3.6 names the conversation store's file-write guard among the watched locks; C-25.3 keeps no runner whose thread could not start, which had left it registered for good and made `close()` raise before the store closed, and has the service's ops, control-loop steps and turn runners open every file they read only as a regular file (admission's read of a turn job's manifest, which admission owns, aside): a FIFO at a stored attachment, a message's text, `catalog.json`, `catalog.lock`, `.git/index` or an attempt's file had held an op, a tick or a runner in `open()`, and `close()` or `Daemon.close()` with it; C-28.1 replaces a stored copy that does not hash right. C-25.3: an op that meets the closed store's refusal fails with it, where `message.status` had answered `unknown` for a message the daemon holds and a cancel `unknown-message` (exit 2); a late runner's containment is refused, where it had reached the closed main store; and a person's stop finds its runner while the control loop adds others, where the lookup had raised on the free-threaded build and left the runner uninterrupted. C-26.6: a later runner keeps the outcome an earlier one recorded, where a replay had overwritten it (a withheld turn then failed instead of being readmitted), a Codex turn whose thread went idle before its provider exited is complete, and a runner adopted after a restart replays a person's stop, where it had sent no interrupt and settled the stopped turn as failed (`ended-without-result`); a message whose attempt ended with no runner to settle it is settled by a replay, where a report `close()` cut short, followed by a finalization before the next daemon's first tick, had left it running for good; a replay reads all of stdout, where it had ended a turn with more than 2 MiB before its result as one with none; and a settlement cut short leaves its message live, where it had left the message failed with its conversation open. C-25.3: the daemon's other waited-for work (the timers, `submit`, `gate.*`, `lanes`, finalization) reads only regular files too, where a FIFO at a login, a guard file, an attempt's record, a prompt, a brief or a v1 roster had held a worker, and `Daemon.close()`, in `open()`; a `submit` naming one held `_submit_lock`, and every submit after it. After a review of these fixes: a roster that is not a regular file is refused rather than read as empty, the text a message, a roster or a probe's output reads as keeps universal newlines, revival falls back to a rename where hard links are refused and syncs its copy, the mirror sweeps its own state files' leftovers, and C-25.3 says which reads are capped, which are bounded by a file's size alone, and that `status.json`'s store check can be raced. C-25.3 caps what a reader reads (it had read a first task past 64 MiB, a line of any length, back to a file's start for a cursor, a registry file or Codex's index whole) and says what still bounds `close()`'s wait and what does not (the walks, a stalled filesystem). C-25.3: a turn's model catalog is merged under a lock of its own, where a merge waiting for another file write held the service lock, and every poll, dispatch claim and adoption with it. C-30.1: a probe of `catalog.lock` is never taken for a run, where one had made a run exit having published nothing, or kept the tick from starting one for an interval. C-23.28: the mirror's writes make their temporaries under names of their own, where a FIFO at revival's fixed name (or at the mirror's own lock) had blocked a pass (and `Timers.stop()`), a link there had truncated the archive, and a temporary replaced before its fsync had blocked the reopen and then been published; revival never replaces a transcript that appeared after its check; and a kept listing no longer makes a linked transcript whose target became a FIFO openable.
- Review of the admission-latency change (2026-09-27, of d04b8b3 and of its fix 5d14f98), two ways it could hold a job back forever where e053b2c placed it: C-6.3's check judges again each lane whose own clock passed its horizon (`capacity.lane_horizons`) or whose override began or ended, every lane of the walk when a cap began or ended or the chain walks further, and no lane the decision does not look at; it refuses only a pin that names another lane now, and reserves on `evaluate`'s decision exactly, each reading's age and label included. C-26.9: turns take `lane:<id>:slot:turn-<n>`, never the `slot:0` a detached job's probe needs, and a turn a probe held is looked at when the probe ends; C-6.10 and C-6.11 say so.
- PR #43 on the desktop line (2026-09-27): the line's descriptor hotfix (C-16.1 as it stood, 0100da6 and its reviews) gives way to #43's C-16.6 and C-16.7, and C-16.1 is the wire format again. The limit is raised to 65536, with fallbacks, and launchd's plist sets it too; the cap follows the limit; each connection has a reader thread of its own; reads have a 60 s idle close; a departed client's reads are dropped but its writes run (the line had dropped every request no pool had started); and `Client.call` asks a busy daemon again, so the gate's poll and the re-send of a lost `submit` or `kill` rely on it alone; the long-poll loops (`wait`, `kill --wait`, `run --wait`) keep their own retry. Kept from the line: C-16.5's lookups pool, the busy answer when no reader can start, and the exit after 300 s of failed `accept`s. New: the reserve grows by two descriptors per turn runner going (its relay connection, C-26.4); the conversation reads join C-16.7's read-only requests; `conversation.events` and `conversation.watch` end when their client leaves and, like `wait`, have a thread for every connection. `daemon.status` reports `descriptors`, which replaces the line's `connections`.
- C-29.12 and C-29.13 (2026-09-28, Max 2026-09-27: "also a keyboard shortcut to search, like cmd+k" and "can you make it more readable? text is small"): ⌘K opens a palette over the main window that finds conversations and sessions by title, workspace, provider and message text, exact title first and then by recent activity within how well each matches, and can start a new conversation; conversation text is set larger (body 16 pt, Claude Code's Large, where the system's is 13), View > Bigger, Smaller and Actual size scale it and the conversation column in Claude Code's zoom steps, and code blocks gain Copy and C-29.8's control to show more lines.
- C-27.5 (2026-09-27, a card that waited unseen): a turn's approval card sat above five queued messages and a failover turn that sorted last although it ran first, while the pinned strip said "Needs your approval" with only a Stop. The strip now carries Review for every pending card; the view scrolls to each new card once its log is read; the sidebar's hand badge opens the card; turns show in the order they began, and queued messages below them. The review of 6e1b505 added begin order (an unblock note had moved below the turn after it), withdrawing the cards of an ended message, waiting for the log before scrolling, and opening only the card the person chose. The independent review of fa30c51 added the provider's `request_id` to the daemon's approval views (two identical requests had joined the wrong approvals), withdrawing a card the daemon no longer lists or whose delivery is unknown, reading the log again on each opening, and a Stop only when it acts.
- C-27.1 (2026-10-01, a card the app could not answer): the turn runner committed an approval's `approval.requested` event, then its approval, then the move to `approval-needed`, three transactions where design §8 says one. The event's commit woke the app's events poll, and the `approval.list` the app sent at once to answer the card could come back without the approval, so the card had no approval id (`tests/frontend/test_core_live.py`, about 3 CI runs in 7 on release/217; a person tapping Review then was told the approval was no longer pending). The three now commit together with the event batch and its watermark (`ConversationStore.add_approvals`), checked at every commit by `tests/unit/test_approval_commit.py`.


- Section 23 adds 55 clauses (C-23.1 to C-23.55) carried forward from the v1 invariant ledger; provenance per clause in `docs/invariant-gaps.md`.
- The eleven conflicts that document raised (C-1 to C-11 there) are all resolved as recommended: C-17.2 gains `-I` and `-D`; C-6.5 gains the isolated-review refusals and becomes the index of refusals; C-14.2 is keyed per lane home; C-2.1 names `~/.claude/settings.json` (install only) and puts the preflight scratch home under the state root; C-17.1 gains `doctor --guard-check` and the permanent `gate`, `sessions`, and `handoff` verbs; C-11.3's Claude comparator sorts the stranded term first; C-10.3 separates `desktop` (Claude) from `app_shadowed` (Codex); C-18.1 reads enabled, unlatched idle and busy lanes through their no-turn sensors.
- Identity binding, from the v1 incident of 2026-09-05: C-1.4, C-10.3, and new C-10.6 and C-10.7. A Claude account is identified by the profile endpoint using the same credential that yields its usage, never by `~/.claude.json`.
- C-9.8 names the literal `admission` window (13:00 EDT).
- C-5.1 and C-5.5 add `SUBFLEET_ROOT=<state root>` as the second half of the containment marker (14:55 EDT).
- C-9.9: Claude home lanes read the macOS keychain item Claude Code writes per config directory; an expired access token heals with one turn (2026-09-06 16:40 EDT, login drive).
- C-9.9, C-11.7: the usage-endpoint sensor (no model turn, paced, Retry-After honoured) and the reserve rule that keeps non-Fable work off the shared weekly window Fable needs (2026-09-06 08:40 EDT, after the Fable-week incident).
- C-11.7a: explicit per-job authorization for unmeasured reserve, scoped to a pinned lane and model and requiring a fresh admission probe (2026-09-20, Max's Opus-capacity instruction and the Microcosm task's implementation request).
- C-2.1, C-10.4: a Codex lane transfer relocates the home under the state root and back; the relocation, not the roster, fences v1 (peer review of the cutover decisions, 18:45 EDT).
- C-4.2 `running` row: an uninspectable guardian decides nothing; a dead guardian's receipt is re-read before containment; a receipt present at finalization wins over an earlier `lost` verdict (17:20 EDT).
- C-5.5, C-5.6, C-5.9: the group and the descendant walk come from one process-table snapshot that also records each live pid's parent, group, and state; bounded settle windows after SIGKILL (`kill_settle_s`) and after the exit receipt (`exit_settle_s`) before an attempt is quarantined (17:05 EDT).
- C-6.12, C-11.2 (with C-4.1's `route` wait and C-6.11's `route` hold): one job whose route cannot be evaluated never stops the admission pass, and a lane pin is resolved once, at submit, against the lanes admission evaluates, narrowed by the job's provider, and stored as the lane id (2026-09-22, the admission stall on email pins; `docs/reports/2026-09-22-admission-route-stall.md`).
- C-16.3, C-17.3, C-17.7, C-15.1, C-9.2: an answer the client never read is not a failure. `submit` and `kill` are re-sent once under the same request id or job, and an outcome still unknown is reported as unknown with the request id. Batch entry ids are always `<batch id>-<n>`. A notice's first line is the job's terminal state and rc, and names paths only for an accepted job. An attempt the daemon signalled is never `ok` (2026-09-24, the lost acknowledgements and the cancelled jobs announced `ok; rc=0`). Retention's unreachable byte budget is a proposal, not yet a clause: `docs/reports/2026-09-24-retention-pins.md`.
- C-23.28 gains its second half, health is not reach: the desktop app reads a session folder only when it loads it, so the mirror spreads new sessions within seconds and reports the copies that arrived after the load (2026-09-24, 19 sessions missing from the sidebar until a relaunch; `docs/reports/2026-09-24-mirror-load-gap.md`). Its flag protocol is specified and model-checked, and a split batch is rolled back (2026-09-25, 84 unarchives stranded in one account while the daemon crash-looped; `docs/reports/2026-09-25-mirror-consistency.md`).
- C-23.28 (2026-09-28, mirror hot-progress review round 3): a successful copy write whose post-write stat fails invalidates the full refresh even when a later copy fails and the merge base stays unchanged; the regression preserves the subsequent unarchive (`docs/reports/2026-09-28-mirror-hot-progress-round3.md`).
- C-23.28 (2026-09-28, mirror hot-progress review round 2): hot rereads retain unread copies’ known owners; refresh epochs track standing copy writes and published archive/star/title decisions; only identified holds enter hot retries, and vanished identities leave them. Cold startup inventories once, unchanged merge bases are not rewritten, and idle hot sidecar records are sampled every 60 seconds. Deterministic embedded-service interleavings and descriptor/listing regressions cover the revised rules (`docs/reports/2026-09-28-mirror-hot-progress-round2.md`).
- C-9.3 (2026-09-25, codex-1, -3 and -6): the provider's refusal of the lane's own key ("Incorrect API key provided", `invalid_api_key`) is `auth-dead`; it had matched the transient `401` pattern, so those lanes kept taking jobs and failing them.
- C-15.1, C-17.1 (2026-09-25, the desktop line merged with the lost-response work above): a conversation turn still writes no notice under the rewritten notice text, and `runs` takes `--request-id` beside `--kind` and `--include-turns`.
- C-5.12 (with C-5.5's census and C-4.2's `starting` row): a running attempt is inspected at most once per `inspect_interval_s` from one process table every attempt shares, each time with a table read since its last and less than an interval before it fell due, and only the inspection that reads waits for `ps`; a failed read, of the table or of its boot identity, is all an interval costs and decides nothing; a census takes each pid's start from its snapshot, and start grace reads the receipts again after its census; `procs` starts `ps` and `sysctl` by `posix_spawn` (2026-09-24, the idle cost of 2026-09-20; review fixes 2026-09-26; `docs/reports/2026-09-24-process-inspection-cost.md`).
- C-5.11 with C-5.12 (2026-09-26, the store-contention line merged with PR #37): a running attempt is inspected from C-5.12's shared table, not by a `ps` of its own; C-5.11 keeps the probe monitor's pacing (under `inspect_interval_s`), the one export statement and the wait hub's reads, and the boot identity follows C-5.12's 5 s rule.
- Reviews of the inspection merge and of PR #37 (2026-09-26, of f628b47): C-5.10 and C-5.11 count a retry of a raised inspection that could not inspect as neither success nor failure, so its backoff grows under contention; C-5.12 names that retry as its one exception to one inspection per interval, and the shared table's reader reads its boot identity before any attempt is given it, so a slow `sysctl` holds one worker, not every inspecting one; the table also says "alive" for a legacy boot timestamp that C-5.3 matches, through one `kern.boottime` read per table, so such an attempt is recorded from it rather than asked about singly; an attempt the daemon launches falls due an interval after its guardian started, so its first table shows the guardian and it reads no table of its own; and C-5.12 says which attempt reads; C-4.2's `starting` row takes, after start grace's census, only a receipt the tick would take. Wording: C-5.11's probe sentences (the leader re-check, the census on every check), C-15.5's last read at a wait's deadline, and "not by a `ps` of its own" in the entry above.
- C-5.8a: a stop normally dumps up to 100 Python thread stacks and exits 1 after 30 s; an independent kernel SIGALRM deadline at 33 s bounds nested signals, faulthandler failure or cancellation, and slow or blocked dumps without needing the GIL or a new thread. Reentrant arms cannot replace the timers. launchd and `subfleet daemon stop` each provide a 40 s SIGKILL backstop only for stops they initiate (2026-09-25 incident; 2026-09-27 round-4 review; `docs/reports/2026-09-25-daemon-stop-wedge.md`).
- C-5.1 (2026-09-27, the daemon's scheduling): the daemon runs at the default QoS (`daemon install` writes launchd `ProcessType` `Interactive`) and the guardian starts each provider under a `utility` QoS clamp its whole tree inherits. `Standard` had clamped the daemon's every thread to `utility`: at load 125 to 173 its `daemon.status` took 1.2 to 10.1 s at the median against 0.04 to 0.06 s at the default QoS, store-lock holds that spent at most 4.3 ms of CPU lasted up to 1.9 s, and the census's socket read took 5 to 8 s against under 1 s (`docs/reports/2026-09-27-daemon-qos.md`).
- C-10.2 (2026-09-27, live): re-enrolling a keychain-token Claude lane runs only its login turn through the enrollment fence. Every runner call had been routed there, including the keychain read that resolves the token, so `lanes enroll claude-quota-…` for a lane Subfleet had disabled as auth-dead raised (`_enrollment_turn() missing … 'cwd' and 'env'`) and no lapsed account could be brought back.
- C-13.1 (2026-09-27, incident; review of c1f95838): two finished review attempts held codex-3 and codex-5 in `finalizing` for 2.5 h and 5 h. A test fixture had left an empty repository (`repo `, no commit) under the reviewer's untracked scratch directory; `git add -A` refused it, and the finalizer retried a salvage that could only fail the same way, every 60 s, holding the lane. Salvage now leaves out a nested repository with no commit, found by listing, not by reading git's messages (the first version ran `add -A --ignore-errors` and so also let through an object git could not write, leaving real work out of the ref unannounced), and names what it left out in the receipt, the attempt's evidence and the job's notice. Finalization bounds its salvage retries per daemon run and records the failure; a held lock file and a full disk count as transient; salvage's git runs under the C locale. Round 3 (2026-09-28, review of cda4c161 and the live daemon.log): salvage and admission read what git prints as the file system names things, and every salvage error is recorded as valid UTF-8 (an error quoting a file name that is not UTF-8 made the receipt unwritable, so finalization raised on every try, the incident again; a branch name that is not UTF-8 raised `UnicodeDecodeError` at admission and in salvage); a retry after a failed salvage starts from a snapshot held under `refs/subfleet-salvage/<job id>-a<seq>-baseline`; live diffs list what their snapshot left out; and git's other full-disk wording, `write error. Out of diskspace` (on 2026-09-22 a job failed at once on it), is transient, the markers read only on git's `error:` and `fatal:` lines.
